研究人员开发出第一个登革热血清型 4 受控人类挑战模型 - Bioengineer.org

Researchers Develop First Dengue Serotype 4 Controlled Human Challenge Model - Bioengineer.org
新闻简报据 Bioengineer.org 于 2026 年 11 月 21 日报道,马里兰大学医学院和人类病毒学研究所的研究人员已开发出第一个成功的 登革病毒 血清型 4 人类攻击模型。这项研究于 2022 年 4 月至 2023 年 12 月在巴尔的摩进行,涉及 14 名健康成年志愿者,他们皮下注射了 95 个空斑形成单位的活攻击病毒。所有参与者都出现了可检测到的病毒血症和轻度至中度症状,包括皮疹、疲劳和头痛。该模型旨在克服研究血清型 4(其传播缓慢且不可预测)的挑战,并促进 疫苗 和 抗病毒药物 测试。研究结果发表在《柳叶刀微生物》上,该研究由军事传染病研究计划和医疗技术企业联盟资助。尽管该试验规模较小且需要进一步验证,但它为黄病毒对策研究提供了重要基础。该研究强调了开发预防性 疫苗 的重要性,因为目前尚无批准的 抗病毒药物 治疗登革热感染的疗法。
查看新闻五要素
时间
该研究于2022年4月至2023年12月期间进行,研究结果于2026年11月21日发布。
地点与对象
该研究在巴尔的摩进行,涉及 14 名健康成年志愿者。
事件
据 Bioengineer.org 于 2026 年 11 月 21 日报道,马里兰大学医学院和人类病毒学研究所的研究人员开发了第一个成功的 登革病毒 血清型 4 人类攻击模型。
规模、影响与风险
所有 14 名参与者均出现了可检测到的病毒血症和轻度至中度登革热症状。该研究旨在评估 疫苗 和 抗病毒药物 的疗效,但没有报告具体病例或死亡人数。该模型旨在克服研究血清型 4 时由于其缓慢且不可预测的循环而面临的挑战。
应对、状态与不确定性
The model was validated through phase 1 dose optimisation and homologous rechallenge trials, with results published in The Lancet Microbe. The study was funded by the Military Infectious Disease Research Program and the Medical Technology Enterprise Consortium. Further validation is required, and participants were isolated in a hotel to prevent community transmission.
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Researchers Develop First Dengue Serotype 4 Controlled Human Challenge Model - Bioengineer.org

News Brief
Researchers at the University of Maryland School of Medicine and the Institute of Human Virology have developed the first successful human challenge model for dengue virus serotype 4, as reported by Bioengineer.org on November 21, 2026. The study, conducted in Baltimore between April 2022 and December 2023, involved 14 healthy adult volunteers who were inoculated with a subcutaneous injection of 95 plaque-forming units of the live challenge virus. All participants developed detectable viremia and mild-to-moderate symptoms, including rash, fatigue, and headaches. The model was designed to overcome the challenges of studying serotype 4, which circulates slowly and unpredictably, and to facilitate vaccine and antiviral testing. Findings were published in The Lancet Microbe, and the study was funded by the Military Infectious Disease Research Program and the Medical Technology Enterprise Consortium. Although the trial was small and requires further validation, it provides a critical foundation for flavivirus countermeasure research. The study emphasized the importance of developing preventative vaccines, as no approved antiviral therapy currently exists for dengue infection.
Original source text (English)
HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER Thursday, November 26, 2026 No Result View All Result Login HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER No Result View All Result No Result View All Result Home NEWS Science News Health Researchers Develop First Dengue Serotype 4 Controlled Human Challenge Model by November 21, 2026 in Health Reading Time: 3 mins read 0 Researchers Develop First Dengue Serotype 4 Controlled Human Challenge Model Share on FacebookShare on TwitterShare on LinkedinShare on RedditShare on Telegram Researchers at the University of Maryland School of Medicine and the Institute of Human Virology have established the first successful human challenge model for dengue virus serotype 4. The experimental clinical model is designed to facilitate and accelerate the evaluation of candidate vaccines and targeted antiviral therapeutics against a viral strain that has historically posed persistent barriers to scientific study. Findings detailing the phase 1 dose optimisation and homologous rechallenge trial were published in The Lancet Microbe. Dengue is a mosquito-borne flavivirus comprising four antigenically distinct serotypes designated DENV-1 through DENV-4. Dengue serotype 4 represents a unique public health and research hurdle because it circulates slowly and unpredictably, frequently with decades elapsing between recognized community outbreaks. Consequently, conventional large-scale field efficacy trials have encountered difficulties assessing vaccine performance, leaving recent phase 3 clinical trials unable to generate reliable efficacy estimates for this specific strain. Conducted in Baltimore between April 2022 and December 2023, the study evaluated healthy adult volunteers to establish viral dynamics and clinical safety parameters. Participants received a single subcutaneous injection containing 95 plaque-forming units of the live challenge virus. Following inoculation, volunteers were closely monitored under strict biosafety and containment precautions to prevent secondary environmental transmission. Participants underwent daily clinical assessments for emergent symptoms and had regular blood collections to measure viral kinetics. To ensure local mosquito populations could not acquire the virus and transmit it within the community, all study participants were accommodated in a local hotel during the window in which active virus remained detectable in their peripheral blood. All 14 enrolled participants subsequently developed detectable viremia alongside mild-to-moderate dengue symptoms. Clinical manifestations followed a consistent presentation across the cohort, with rash and fatigue developing in every participant, followed in frequency by headaches. To map immune mechanisms comprehensively, investigators paired clinical tracking with longitudinal immunological and transcriptomic profiling. After the initial viral challenge, participants mounted robust primary immune defenses characterized by significant antibody responses, marked expansion of antibody-producing cells, and widespread upregulation of antiviral genes. The newly validated platform builds upon previous clinical models previously developed for dengue serotypes 1 and 3, forming part of a broader consortium effort focused on establishing human challenge frameworks for all four circulating serotypes. Although the trial was conducted in a relatively small cohort of healthy adult volunteers and requires further validation, the established model provides a critical clinical foundation for ongoing flavivirus countermeasure research. According to study principal investigator Kirsten E. Lyke, MD, Professor of Medicine at the Center for Vaccine Development and Global Health, the dengue virus ranks among the World Health Organization’s top ten global health threats. Lyke cautioned that a recent outbreak in Florida represents only an initial harbinger of the geographic spread of dengue projected to occur across the United States by 2080. Because medical science currently has no approved antiviral therapy to treat dengue infection, developing preventative vaccines remains critically important for protecting human populations both immediately and over the coming decades. Lyke highlighted that controlled infection models allow investigators to test therapeutics, biologic agents, and vaccines in people so that researchers can focus energy and resources on advancing products with confirmed efficacy. The phase 1 dose optimisation and homologous rechallenge trial was funded by the Military Infectious Disease Research Program and the Medical Technology Enterprise Consortium. 佛罗里达州登革热病例已达 651 例,专家警告后院蚊子滋生

Florida Dengue Cases Reach 651, Experts Warn About Mosquitoes Breeding in Backyards
新闻简报2026 年,佛罗里达州经历了一场严重的登革热 暴发,12 个县报告了 651 例本地感染病例,这是美国大陆数十年来最大规模的病例。暴发集中在坦帕湾周边,特别是希尔斯伯勒县(截至11月14日)有487例,皮尼拉斯县有116例。卫生专家警告说,由于漏报,真实感染人数可能更高,因为有些病例症状轻微或无症状。佛罗里达州卫生部和疾病预防控制中心强调清除蚊子繁殖场所的重要性,例如花盆碟、堵塞的排水沟和雨桶,并建议使用 EPA 注册的驱虫剂,并避免使用某些药物,如阿司匹林和布洛芬。虽然大多数感染都是轻微的,但可能会出现出血、液体渗漏或休克等严重病例,需要立即就医。希尔斯伯勒县已将当地紧急状态延长至 11 月 26 日,突显了持续存在的公共卫生问题。
查看新闻五要素
时间
截至 2026 年 11 月 14 日,每周更新表明新病例有所下降(最近报告周新增 27 例病例,低于上一份报告的 100 例)。
地点与对象
佛罗里达州,特别集中在坦帕湾周围,截至 2026 年 11 月 14 日,希尔斯伯勒县报告了 487 例病例,皮尼拉斯县报告了 116 例病例。
事件
据佛罗里达州卫生部报告,佛罗里达州登革热 暴发 不断增长,2026 年,12 个县记录了 651 例本地感染病例。暴发 被描述为美国大陆几十年来最大的。
规模、影响与风险
暴发 已超过佛罗里达州此前在 2023 年创下的 199 例病例记录,感染人数远远超过之前的水平。卫生专家警告说,由于轻微或无症状感染,报告的病例可能低估了 暴发 的真实范围。疾病预防控制中心建议休息、液体和对乙酰氨基酚来控制症状,同时建议不要使用阿司匹林和布洛芬,因为存在出血风险。
应对、状态与不确定性
希尔斯伯勒县已将当地紧急状态延长至 11 月 26 日,疾病预防控制中心 (CDC) 提供了有关检查室外空间、清除繁殖地和使用适当驱虫剂的指导。然而,由于报告延误和无症状感染者,确切的病例数可能被低估。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Florida Dengue Cases Reach 651, Experts Warn About Mosquitoes Breeding in Backyards

News Brief
In 2026, Florida has experienced a significant dengue outbreak, with 651 locally acquired cases reported across 12 counties, marking the largest in the continental United States in decades. The outbreak is concentrated around Tampa Bay, particularly in Hillsborough County, which accounts for 487 cases, and Pinellas County, with 116 cases as of November 14. Health experts warn that the true number of infections may be higher due to underreporting, as some cases cause mild symptoms or are asymptomatic. The Florida Department of Health and the CDC emphasize the importance of removing mosquito breeding sites, such as flowerpot saucers, clogged gutters, and rain barrels, and recommend using EPA-registered insect repellents and avoiding certain medications like aspirin and ibuprofen. While most infections are mild, severe cases involving bleeding, fluid leakage, or shock can occur, requiring immediate medical attention. Hillsborough County has extended its local state of emergency through November 26, highlighting the ongoing public health concern.
Original source text (English)
Florida homeowners may have more to worry about than weeds and pests hiding in their gardens. A growing dengue outbreak has brought new attention to the small pools of standing water where disease-carrying mosquitoes can breed, including places people might overlook during routine yard maintenance. According to the latest Florida Department of Health surveillance figures , the state has recorded 651 locally acquired dengue cases across 12 counties in 2026. The outbreak is the largest reported in the continental United States in decades, with infections far exceeding Florida's previous annual record of 199 cases in 2023. The outbreak has been concentrated around Tampa Bay, with Hillsborough County accounting for 487 cases and neighboring Pinellas County reporting 116 as of November 14. Although the latest weekly figures suggest infections may be slowing, Hillsborough County has extended its local state of emergency through November 26. Health experts also warn that official case counts may underestimate the true number of infections. For residents, the concern extends beyond neighborhoods with confirmed cases. The mosquito species responsible for much of dengue transmission can reproduce around ordinary homes, turning flowerpot saucers, clogged gutters, and forgotten outdoor containers into potential breeding sites. Embedded media follows; please allow a moment to load. Unlike mosquitoes associated with large ponds or marshes, Aedes aegypti, a major mosquito species responsible for dengue transmission, is well adapted to living near people. It lays eggs along the inside walls of containers that collect water, making residential properties suitable places for reproduction. Kevin Sukovich, a licensed pest control operator who owns Clermont-based Webenex, told the Clermont Sun that homeowners play an important role in controlling these mosquitoes, even when county crews are spraying public areas. "The county can spray the streets, but this mosquito breeds in a bottle cap of water behind your grill," Sukovich said. "Nobody owns that part. The homeowner does." The CDC recommends inspecting outdoor spaces at least once a week. Birdbaths, buckets, children's toys, discarded tires, and flowerpot saucers should be emptied and scrubbed to remove mosquito eggs. Simply pouring out the water may leave eggs attached to the container. Gardeners should pay particular attention to areas where water collects unnoticed. Rain barrels need tight-fitting covers or fine mesh screens, while gutters should be kept clear so water can drain. Water-holding plants and containers also deserve attention, especially in shaded sections of the yard. Removing breeding sites is an important first step, but it does not eliminate every mosquito already living around a property. According to CDC mosquito-control guidance , adult mosquitoes can shelter in dark, humid locations, including beneath patio furniture and around garages. Targeted insecticide treatments may help control them, although products must be used according to their labels. For water that cannot be drained or covered, the agency recommends appropriately labeled mosquito larvicides. These target immature mosquitoes before they become flying adults. Homeowners should select products intended for the particular location rather than applying pesticides indiscriminately around ornamental plants, wildlife habitats, or water features. Personal protection matters, too. Aedes mosquitoes can bite during daylight hours, meaning gardeners are not necessarily safe just because they avoid going outside at dusk. EPA-registered insect repellents, long sleeves, and well-maintained window screens can reduce exposure while residents work outdoors or relax on patios. Dengue can cause fever, severe headaches, pain behind the eyes, muscle and joint pain, nausea, and rashes. However, not everyone who becomes infected develops noticeable symptoms. Health experts caution that reported cases may underestimate the outbreak's true extent because some infections cause mild symptoms or no symptoms at all. Reporting delays can also make it difficult to measure the outbreak while cases are being investigated. According to WUSF's November 20 report , Florida recorded 27 additional locally acquired cases during the latest reporting week, down from 100 in the preceding report. Although most people with symptomatic dengue recover, the infection can occasionally progress to severe illness involving bleeding, fluid leakage, or shock. The CDC advises seeking emergency medical care for warning signs such as persistent vomiting, severe abdominal pain, or unusual bleeding, particularly after a fever begins to subside. There is no specific antiviral treatment for dengue. The CDC recommends rest, adequate fluids, and acetaminophen to help manage fever and pain. People who have or may have dengue should avoid aspirin and ibuprofen because these medications can increase the risk of bleeding. Anyone who suspects they have dengue should contact a healthcare professional for evaluation and guidance. For Florida homeowners, preventing mosquito bites begins with paying attention to the places where mosquitoes reproduce. A forgotten bucket, clogged gutter, or water-filled flowerpot saucer can provide breeding conditions even in an otherwise well-maintained yard. With hundreds of locally acquired dengue infections already reported this year, a weekly inspection of outdoor spaces is a simple precaution worth taking. Removing standing water, maintaining screens, and using appropriate insect repellent can help reduce exposure while residents continue enjoying their gardens. Want more articles like this one? 气候和水文信号改善了东北亚的跨国登革热风险预测 - Bioengineer.org

Climate and Hydrological Signals Improve Cross-National Dengue Risk Predictions in Northeast Asia - Bioengineer.org
新闻简报Bioengineer.org 于 2026 年 11 月 24 日发表的一项研究探讨了气候和水文信号如何改善日本和韩国的跨国登革热风险预测。这项研究由 Suhyun Han 和 Jong-Hun Kim 进行,旨在比依赖历史趋势和季节性模式的传统模型更准确地预测每月输入的登革热病例。通过将国际旅行量、海面温度、干旱措施和病毒血清型动态等因素整合到机器学习框架中,该团队证明环境信号可显着减少预测误差并提高方向准确性。该研究使用了2005年1月至2025年6月的数据,由于2019年冠状病毒病大流行的影响,不包括2020年1月至2022年12月期间。最终的堆叠集成模型优于传统方法,在日本和韩国有显着改进。然而,该研究指出了局限性,例如使用首都数据来代表国家干旱状况,这可能无法反映地方环境多样性。这项研究强调了纳入客观环境指标的重要性,以更好地预测非流行地区的登革热风险,因为输入性感染可能会引发当地的 传播 循环。
查看新闻五要素
时间
该研究发表于2026年11月24日。分析时间序列纳入了2005年1月至2025年6月的月度数据,不包括由于2019年冠状病毒病大流行的影响而导致的2020年1月至2022年12月期间的数据。
地点与对象
该研究的重点是日本和韩国,它们是非流行的温带国家。它还提到了印度尼西亚、菲律宾、泰国和越南等登革热流行目的地,这些地方是登革热的常见传播地。
事件
研究人员评估了环境、水文和病毒流行病学指标是否可以比传统模型更可靠地预测日本和韩国每月输入的登革热风险。研究表明,这些指标显着提高了预测准确性。
规模、影响与风险
该研究强调,非流行地区的输入性登革热感染可导致本土 传播,正如 2014 年东京 暴发 中所见,有 162 例国内感染病例。它还指出,传统模型通常依赖于不一致的病例通知,从而给预测带来了不确定性。
应对、状态与不确定性
研究人员利用环境和水文指标开发了一个堆叠集成模型,其性能优于参考模型。然而,该研究承认存在局限性,例如使用单一首都城市数据来了解全国干旱状况,这可能会忽视地方环境变化。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Climate and Hydrological Signals Improve Cross-National Dengue Risk Predictions in Northeast Asia - Bioengineer.org

News Brief
A study published on November 24, 2026, by Bioengineer.org explored how climate and hydrological signals can improve cross-national dengue risk predictions in Japan and South Korea. The research, conducted by Suhyun Han and Jong-Hun Kim, aimed to forecast monthly imported dengue cases more accurately than traditional models that rely on historical trends and seasonal patterns. By integrating factors like international travel volume, sea surface temperatures, drought measures, and viral serotype dynamics into a machine learning framework, the team demonstrated that environmental signals significantly reduce prediction errors and enhance directional accuracy. The study used data from January 2005 to June 2025, excluding the period from January 2020 to December 2022 due to the impact of the coronavirus disease 2019 pandemic. The final stacked ensemble model outperformed conventional approaches, with notable improvements in Japan and South Korea. However, the study noted limitations, such as the use of capital-city data to represent national drought conditions, which may not capture subnational environmental diversity. This research underscores the importance of incorporating objective environmental indicators to better predict dengue risk in non-endemic regions, where imported infections can spark local transmission cycles.
Original source text (English)
HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER Thursday, November 26, 2026 No Result View All Result Login HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER No Result View All Result No Result View All Result Home NEWS Science News Health Climate and Hydrological Signals Improve Cross-National Dengue Risk Predictions in Northeast Asia by November 24, 2026 in Health Reading Time: 6 mins read 0 Climate and Hydrological Signals Improve Cross-National Dengue Risk Predictions in Northeast Asia Share on FacebookShare on TwitterShare on LinkedinShare on RedditShare on Telegram In non-endemic temperate countries, imported dengue infections pose an ongoing public health challenge because returning travelers carrying the virus can spark local transmission cycles whenever competent mosquito vectors and permissive environmental conditions align. In an extensive cross-national study published in Tropical Medicine and Health, researchers Suhyun Han and Jong-Hun Kim evaluated whether upstream environmental, hydrological, and viral epidemiological indicators could forecast monthly imported dengue risk in Japan and the Republic of Korea more reliably than models relying strictly on past temporal trends and recurring seasonal curves. By integrating international travel volume, Pacific and Indian Ocean sea surface temperature indices, source-country drought measures, and regional viral serotype dynamics into a multi-algorithm machine learning framework, the investigators established that environmental signals substantially reduce case prediction error and enhance directional accuracy across both nations. Dengue is an acute mosquito-borne viral infection transmitted predominantly by Aedes aegypti and Aedes albopictus mosquitoes, with global transmission heavily concentrated across tropical and subtropical environments. Globalization, international mobility, and modern commercial transportation networks have substantially magnified the introduction of dengue virus into temperate, non-endemic settings. Infected travelers frequently return while incubating the pathogen or during an active viremic phase, while commercial trade routes can unintentionally disperse competent mosquito vectors or desiccation-resistant eggs stored in commodities such as used tires. When imported infections enter a region hosting established vector populations during warm seasons, autochthonous transmission can emerge, as demonstrated by the 2014 Tokyo outbreak that resulted in 162 domestically acquired dengue cases. In South Korea and Japan, imported dengue cases continue to be detected annually, exhibiting pronounced seasonal peaks in August in South Korea and September in Japan, mirroring country-specific summer outbound travel patterns to endemic destinations such as Indonesia, the Philippines, Thailand, and Vietnam. Conventional surveillance and predictive modeling approaches in non-endemic regions often rely on case notification counts reported by source countries or short-term regional data. However, the completeness, timeliness, diagnostic infrastructure, and reporting consistency of dengue surveillance systems vary considerably across endemic nations, introducing substantial reporting uncertainty into downstream forecasting models. To circumvent this vulnerability, the researchers constructed a modeling framework that does not rely on foreign government case notifications, turning instead to objective, large-scale physical and biological indicators. Candidate environmental predictors encompassed sea surface temperature anomalies across the Pacific Ocean, including the Oceanic Niño Index, Niño 3, Niño 3.4, and Niño 4 indices, alongside the Indian Ocean Dipole Mode Index. Atmospheric monsoon indices were integrated through the Western North Pacific, Indian, Australian, South American, and Webster-Yang monsoon indices, while hydrological stress in source nations was captured via the Standardized Precipitation-Evapotranspiration Index across one-, three-, six-, and twelve-month timescales for the capital cities of Indonesia, the Philippines, Thailand, and Vietnam. To capture broader viral evolutionary pressures, the team also formulated an indicator of viral serotype dominance persistence, defined as the cumulative number of consecutive years in which a specific dengue virus serotype remained dominant within the Western Pacific Region, derived from World Health Organization surveillance records. The analytical time series incorporated monthly data spanning January 2005 through June 2025. To safeguard model calibration against extreme systemic disruption, the three-year window from January 2020 through December 2022 was excluded from both model development and evaluation due to severe coronavirus disease 2019 travel curbs and abnormal imported case fluctuations. The remaining historical sequence was divided into a 180-month training dataset covering January 2005 to December 2019 and a prospective test evaluation dataset running from January 2023 to June 2025. The core target outcome was formulated by adding a pseudocount of one to monthly imported case counts, dividing by official monthly outbound traveler volumes, and log-transforming the resulting travel-volume-adjusted risk ratio. Predictor lags were systematically identified prior to algorithm training using negative binomial regression models with an offset for log-transformed outbound travel volume. Candidate lag search windows were intentionally constrained to realistic reporting timelines, applying a minimum two-month lag across ocean, atmospheric, and hydrological indices to accommodate data dissemination schedules, while evaluating Pacific ocean temperatures across two- to nine-month intervals and fixing serotype persistence to a six-month lag based on earlier empirical protocols. The variable specification that minimized the Akaike Information Criterion was selected for each factor, culminating in 72 distinct candidate predictor sets per country. These sets combined calendar month and secular year trends with systematically varying blocks of climate, monsoon, drought, and serotype parameters, providing the structural input for four supervised learning algorithms: Elastic Net regression, Random Forest, Support Vector Machine, and eXtreme Gradient Boosting. Model training was conducted exclusively on the 2005-2019 dataset using identical ten-fold cross-validation folds fixed by a constant random seed across all algorithms, evaluating up to 36 prespecified hyperparameter combinations per algorithm. To evaluate whether non-temporal predictors generated genuine forecasting value, the authors established a trend-and-seasonality-only reference model fitted via linear regression using identical cross-validation splits. Once candidate models generated predictions on the log-risk scale, values were exponentiated and multiplied by a Duan smearing factor derived from out-of-fold training residuals to counteract retransformation bias before scaling back to monthly case counts against outbound passenger numbers. In the training phase, Random Forest, Support Vector Machine, and eXtreme Gradient Boosting achieved lower cross-validated root mean squared errors than Elastic Net in both countries and were subsequently assembled as base learners into a stacked ensemble architecture. Evaluating meta-learners across thirty hyperparameter sets demonstrated that an Elastic Net meta-learner minimized cross-validated error compared to a Random Forest meta-learner, securing its selection as the final ensemble engine. When assessed against the held-out test dataset spanning January 2023 to June 2025, the final stacked ensemble substantially outperformed the reference model across all evaluated performance domains. In Japan, the stacked ensemble reduced root mean squared error from 23.875 cases in the reference model down to 7.220 cases, while lowering mean absolute error from 19.779 to 5.577. Concurrently, mean directional accuracy, which tracks the proportion of consecutive months in which predicted and observed counts move in identical directions, increased from 48.276 percent to 65.517 percent, and high-risk discrimination measured by the area under the receiver operating characteristic curve rose from 0.863 to 0.876. In South Korea, root mean squared error fell from 28.812 to 6.313 cases, mean absolute error dropped from 24.078 to 5.113, directional accuracy climbed from 44.828 percent to 65.517 percent, and the discrimination curve expanded from 0.742 to 0.756. Linear calibration slopes in both countries shifted noticeably closer to the ideal value of one, improving from 0.361 to 0.674 in Japan and from 0.218 to 0.736 in South Korea. The study also verified model stability through expanding-window rolling-origin temporal validation across the historical period from 2016 to 2019, where predictor selection and ensemble construction were re-executed using only data available prior to each annual forecast window. In these temporal validation trials, the stacked ensemble maintained the lowest pooled root mean squared error in both nations, dropping from 16.388 to 13.250 in Japan and from 29.430 to 9.505 in South Korea relative to the reference framework. Furthermore, the ensemble attained the highest directional accuracy in Japan at 89.4 percent and tied for top performance in South Korea at 72.3 percent. Inspection of normalized meta-learner coefficients highlighted distinct country-specific weighting architectures: in Japan, all three base learners contributed meaningfully, led by eXtreme Gradient Boosting at 67.2 percent, Support Vector Machine at 18.3 percent, and Random Forest at 14.5 percent. In South Korea, weighting proved highly concentrated, with eXtreme Gradient Boosting accounting for 87.3 percent of meta-learner importance, Support Vector Machine representing 12.7 percent, and Random Forest dropping to zero. Standardized variable importance metrics and block contribution analyses revealed that large-scale Pacific Ocean sea surface temperature indices provided the most robust and consistent predictive signal across both countries. Specifically, the six-month-lagged Oceanic Niño Index ranked highest among non-temporal predictors across all base models in Japan, while the five-month-lagged Niño 3 index consistently led predictor importance in South Korea. Drought conditions in endemic source countries, particularly the Standardized Precipitation-Evapotranspiration Index for Thailand, also demonstrated prominent predictive utility, ranking among top variables in several base specifications. Conversely, atmospheric monsoon indices and regional serotype persistence provided smaller, less uniform improvements across model permutations. Multiple sensitivity analyses, which explored extending lag windows by one month, utilizing Poisson regression for lag selection, shifting outcome pseudocounts to 0.5, and altering high-risk percentiles from the 75th to the 70th and 80th percentiles, confirmed the consistent retention of Pacific sea surface temperatures and Thailand hydrological indicators without compromising predictive stability. Despite these demonstrable predictive gains, the authors detailed several critical operational and epidemiological limitations. The study was restricted to point predictions without generating probabilistic forecasts or uncertainty intervals, meaning that probabilistic calibration and prediction interval coverage could not be verified. Furthermore, the test period was limited to thirty post-pandemic months, during which international travel was steadily recovering, and lag selection was performed once across the full training dataset rather than being iteratively nested inside cross-validation splits. Data resolution constraints also prevented the identification of localized outbreak clusters, family travel groups, or destination-specific flight volumes, as South Korean destination-specific passenger records were discontinued following the abolition of outbound departure cards in 2006. In addition, using single capital-city grid coordinates as proxies for national drought conditions overlooks broader subnational environmental heterogeneity. To facilitate transparent exploration and scenario testing, the researchers deployed an interactive R Shiny web application, while emphasizing that operational deployment in public health agencies will require automated data feeds, expanded post-pandemic observation windows, and individual-level travel records. 登革热比之前想象的更为普遍 - 雅虎

Dengue more widespread than previously thought - Yahoo
新闻简报A new study reveals that dengue is more widespread than previously believed, with global cases rising 34-fold between 1990 and 2024. Researchers, presenting at the American Society of Tropical Medicine and Hygiene meeting, found 73.9 million cases, 10.6 million more than previously recorded. The disease, spread by Aedes mosquitoes, causes severe symptoms and can be fatal, particularly in low and middle-income countries. In Bangladesh, 168,933 people were hospitalised with dengue and 510 died this year, surpassing previous years. However, under-reporting is a major issue, with the real total likely 10 times higher. The study highlights the need for better data collection and improved vector control measures. The Wolbachia method, which reduces dengue transmission by infecting mosquitoes, has shown promise in Brazil and Indonesia. Despite these efforts, challenges like fuel shortages and limited medical resources continue to hinder effective responses to dengue outbreaks.
查看新闻五要素
时间
该研究的结果于 2026 年在马里兰州举行的美国热带医学和卫生学会年会上公布。数据跨度从 1990 年到 2024 年,最新病例报告于 2026 年。该研究是预印本,尚未经过同行评审。
地点与对象
该分析涵盖全球数据,特别提到了孟加拉国、马来西亚、古巴和斯里兰卡。今年,孟加拉国有 168,933 人因登革热住院治疗,其中 510 人死亡,而斯里兰卡则报告了 110,431 例病例和 82 人死亡。截至上个月底,马来西亚已有 79,600 例感染病例,超过 2025 年的 55,399 例。截至 2026 年 10 月,古巴已有 15,859 例病例。该研究还注意到登革热在南亚(包括农村地区)的传播。
事件
研究人员警告说,登革热的传播范围比之前认为的更为广泛,这是基于对1990年至2024年全球数据的分析。该研究在美洲热带医学与卫生学会会议上发表,发现全球共有73.9百万病例,比之前记录的病例多出10.6百万。重症登革热可能致命,并对低收入和中等收入国家造成重大负担。
规模、影响与风险
Global dengue incidence rose 34-fold between 1990 and 2024, with an average annual increase of 9.1 per cent. In Bangladesh, 168,933 hospitalisations and 510 deaths were reported this year, surpassing 2024 and 2025. The real total is estimated to be 10 times higher due to under-reporting. The severe form of the disease can be fatal, and it has a significant impact on health systems in low and middle-income countries.
应对、状态与不确定性
该研究是预印本,正在接受同行评审。研究人员正在通过将无数来源与建模相结合来解决数据差距。沃尔巴克氏体方法已在多个国家用于减少登革热 传播,在巴西和印度尼西亚取得了可喜的成果。然而,报告不足和数据收集不一致仍然是挑战,特别是在孟加拉国和其他地区。该论文尚未在期刊上发表,登革热的真正影响可能被低估。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Dengue more widespread than previously thought - Yahoo

News Brief
A new study reveals that dengue is more widespread than previously believed, with global cases rising 34-fold between 1990 and 2024. Researchers, presenting at the American Society of Tropical Medicine and Hygiene meeting, found 73.9 million cases, 10.6 million more than previously recorded. The disease, spread by Aedes mosquitoes, causes severe symptoms and can be fatal, particularly in low and middle-income countries. In Bangladesh, 168,933 people were hospitalised with dengue and 510 died this year, surpassing previous years. However, under-reporting is a major issue, with the real total likely 10 times higher. The study highlights the need for better data collection and improved vector control measures. The Wolbachia method, which reduces dengue transmission by infecting mosquitoes, has shown promise in Brazil and Indonesia. Despite these efforts, challenges like fuel shortages and limited medical resources continue to hinder effective responses to dengue outbreaks.
Original source text (English)
Dengue is much more widespread than previously thought, researchers have warned, raising concerns that the threat has been underestimated and resources misallocated. An analysis of global data from 1990 to 2024 found that there were 73.9 million cases of the mosquito-borne disease – 10.6 million more than previously captured. "Collectively these data show that dengue is expanding faster, more consistently and more intensely than previously thought," the authors from institutions in the UK, the US, South Africa and Singapore wrote. Dengue, nicknamed breakbone fever, is spread by the Aedes mosquito and it can cause intense muscle, joint and bone aches. The severe form of the disease can be fatal, and it poses a significant burden on low and middle-income countries. The research, presented at the annual meeting of the American Society of Tropical Medicine and Hygiene in Maryland, comes as countries including Bangladesh, Malaysia and Cuba are being hit hard by outbreaks of the disease. The paper , not yet published in a journal, found that global incidence rose 34-fold between 1990 and 2024, an average annual jump of 9.1 per cent. Cases rose most rapidly in regions where the virus has recently emerged – Africa, the Middle East and South Asia – and more slowly in Southeast Asia, where it's been established for decades. Researchers found the missing cases by combing through uncounted sources, like ministry of health PDFs and graphics that never made it into global datasets. They then combined this with modelling, to pull together a single database that tracks 35 years of cases by month, allowing scientists to test new hypotheses. Dengue is spread by the Aedes mosquito - iStockphoto/Pawich Sattalerd "We've addressed the patchy data landscape for dengue," Dr Ahyoung Lim, an epidemiologist and modelling expert at the London School of Hygiene and Tropical Medicine and co-author of the paper, told the Telegraph from the Maryland conference. "Previously, we didn't have temporally consistent global dengue statistics. This has been problematic, because consistent data is crucial for answering questions related to the dengue burden, evaluating interventions and even just understanding the drivers of transmission," she added. The paper is currently a preprint – it's undergoing the peer-review process, having been accepted by a journal. But Prof Ooi Eng Eong, a professor in emerging infectious diseases at Duke NUS in Singapore but not involved in the study, said it will "form a focal reference point on the global dengue burden". "Underestimation of the annual incidence of cases... undermines disease control efforts," he told the Telegraph . "Providing a number to the growth of the problem is one of the key steps towards appropriate allocation of resources to prevent dengue." Dr Lim said the study doesn't account for under-reporting of dengue – for instance, mild and asymptomatic cases can be missed if people don't go to see a doctor, while some hospital systems are disconnected. This has been a major issue in Bangladesh, where health workers also battling a deadly outbreak of measles are under huge strain. A municipal worker fumigates a market area during a dengue prevention campaign in Colombo - AFP via Getty Images/ISHARA S. KODIKARA So far this year 168,933 people have been hospitalised with dengue and 510 have died in the country, according to the ministry of health – surpassing 2024 and 2025, which both saw roughly 100,000 infections, but still well below the record of 320,000 cases and 1,700 deaths in 2023. But patchy reporting means the real total is "10 times higher", said Prof Kabirul Bashar, a medical entomologist at Jahangirnagar University in Bangladesh and member of the World Health Organization (WHO)'s southeast Asia dengue advisory group. "We have more than 7,000 hospitals and small clinics, but [many do not] report their dengue cases to the government's health emergency centre. Many dengue cases are treated at home, so are not counted by anyone," he told The Telegraph. The virus is thriving in the South Asian country because of a combination of warming temperatures , rapid unplanned urbanisation, and patchy vector control measures – and although the dengue-carrying Aedes mosquito is traditionally a city-dweller, it is now spreading across rural regions. "We've failed at vector control, and now the Aedes mosquito is breeding across the country," Prof Bashar said. "It's becoming a bigger and bigger problem. "In Bangladesh in November and December, dengue cases are usually very low, but now we're forecasting a historical [high]... Dengue has taken over the whole country, not only the city but also the rural areas." New hope As countries across the world battle to control mosquito populations, one of the more promising tools to combat the insects has been Wolbachia . Wolbachia, a naturally occurring bacteria, is found in a range of insects, though not the Aedes mosquito. But when the mosquito is infected with the bacteria, it inhibits the dengue virus's ability to replicate. In effect, the bacteria and the virus are competing for resources – and Wolbachia comes out on top. Countries from Columbia to Laos have released mosquitoes infected with the bacteria – when these insects mate, they pass the Wolbachia onto their offspring. The aim is that, after several generations, the majority of insects in any given area carry the bacteria, which then helps to suppress the spread of disease. On Saturday, also at the annual meeting of the American Society of Tropical Medicine and Hygiene, researchers said the Wolbachia method had reduced dengue infections among children by 69 per cent in Brazil – and reduced chikungunya, which is spread by the same mosquitoes, by 73 per cent. The four-year randomised control trial , which has not yet been published in a peer-reviewed journal, included 5,750 schoolchildren aged six to 11 in areas with and without Wolbachia-carrying mosquitoes. Researchers analysed blood samples to compare infection rates. "These results provide powerful new evidence that Wolbachia can reduce dengue transmission, even during major epidemics," said Dr Luciano Moreira, a special advisor to the World Mosquito Program, which led the work. He added that it builds on previous research. A randomised control trial published in 2020 in Indonesia found dengue cases dropped by 77 per cent in areas of Yogyakarta city where Wolbachia mosquitoes were released, while results in 2023 showed incidence fell by at least 94 per cent in three Colombian cities. Experts hope that interventions like this, along with new dengue vaccines , could eventually help turn the tide against a disease that's been described as a rising global threat. Bangladesh isn't the only place to have a major dengue outbreak this year: government data shows that nearby Sri Lanka has seen 110,431 cases and 82 deaths , with peak transmission was in July, while Malaysia reported 79,600 infections by the end of last month – far surpassing the 55,399 recorded in 2025. Meanwhile in Cuba, the Global Dengue Observatory estimated there were 15,859 cases through October – 10.6 times higher than the expected level. Fuel shortages, blackouts and shortages of basic medicines have affected all aspects of the response. And in neighbouring Florida, an 80-year-old woman called Gay Small died in September as the virus gains a new foothold in the state – there have been at least 252 locally acquired cases this year. "I think the [new figures] are both a warning and a wake-up call," said Dr Lim. "Malaria is also a mosquito-borne disease and shares many things in common with dengue... but they are seeing opposite trajectories. Malaria has seen a decline over past decades, but dengue is emerging rapidly. So we do need more focus on it." View five news elements
Time
The study's findings were presented at the annual meeting of the American Society of Tropical Medicine and Hygiene in Maryland in 2026. The data spans from 1990 to 2024, with the most recent cases reported in 2026. The research is a preprint and not yet peer-reviewed.
Location and population
The analysis covers global data, with specific mentions of Bangladesh, Malaysia, Cuba, and Sri Lanka. In Bangladesh, 168,933 people were hospitalised with dengue and 510 died this year, while Sri Lanka reported 110,431 cases and 82 deaths. Malaysia had 79,600 infections by the end of last month, surpassing 2025's 55,399. Cuba had 15,859 cases through October 2026. The study also notes the spread of dengue in South Asia, including rural areas.
Event
Researchers warned that dengue is more widespread than previously thought, based on an analysis of global data from 1990 to 2024. The study, presented at the American Society of Tropical Medicine and Hygiene meeting, found 73.9 million cases globally, 10.6 million more than previously captured. The severe form of dengue can be fatal and poses a significant burden on low and middle-income countries.
Scale, impact and risk
Global dengue incidence rose 34-fold between 1990 and 2024, with an average annual increase of 9.1 per cent. In Bangladesh, 168,933 hospitalisations and 510 deaths were reported this year, surpassing 2024 and 2025. The real total is estimated to be 10 times higher due to under-reporting. The severe form of the disease can be fatal, and it has a significant impact on health systems in low and middle-income countries.
Response, status and uncertainty
The study is a preprint and undergoing peer review. Researchers are addressing data gaps by combining uncounted sources with modelling. The Wolbachia method has been used in several countries to reduce dengue transmission, with promising results in Brazil and Indonesia. However, under-reporting and inconsistent data collection remain challenges, particularly in Bangladesh and other regions. The paper is not yet published in a journal, and the real impact of dengue is likely underestimated.

登革热气候研究符合当前负担,但不符合长期趋势 - Bioengineer.org

Dengue Climate Research Aligns With Current Burden but Not Long-Term Trends - Bioengineer.org
新闻简报本新闻报道讨论了对 登革热 的全球科学文献及其与气候因素的关系的文献计量分析。这项研究于 2008 年至 2026 年 5 月期间进行,发现虽然自 2020 年以来对登革热 传播 气象驱动因素的研究显着增长,但它与当前的疾病负担或 发病率 发病率的长期趋势不一致。这项研究对 290 种出版物进行了评估,结果显示,尽管登革热在 90 个国家和地区广泛传播,但科学生产力主要集中在少数资源丰富的国家。研究结果凸显了研究工作的地理不匹配,导致一些具有高潜力和严重公共卫生负担的地区在国际科学出版物中的代表性不足。该研究强调了气候变量与登革热传播动态之间复杂的相互作用,强调需要更公平的研究分配以有效应对全球健康挑战。

时间
该研究于2008年至2026年5月期间进行,最终分析于2026年5月22日完成。文章发表日期为2026年11月23日。

该研究涉及全球范围,参与作者来自 66 个国家。分析重点是 登革热 及其与气候因素的关联,特别是在热带和亚热带地区。
事件
通过文献计量分析来评估有关 登革热 的全球科学文献及其与气候因素的关系。研究发现,虽然2020年后对登革热传播气象驱动因素的研究有所增加,但与疾病负担的长期趋势并不相符。
规模、影响与风险
该研究强调,尽管登革热 传播 严重且其他地区面临严重的公共卫生负担,但研究生产力仍然集中在少数资源丰富的国家。报告指出,登革热 是传播最迅速的蚊媒病毒感染之一,在 90 个国家和地区记录到活跃的 传播。
应对、状态与不确定性
该研究是一项全面的文献计量调查,使用了 VOSviewer、CiteSpace 和 bibliometrix 等工具。它确定了 290 篇出版物作为最终的分析语料库。研究结果表明研究成果与疾病负担之间不匹配,并且没有明确说明风险水平。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Dengue Climate Research Aligns With Current Burden but Not Long-Term Trends - Bioengineer.org

News Brief
This news report discusses a bibliometric analysis of global scientific literature on dengue fever and its relationship with climate factors. The study, conducted between 2008 and May 2026, found that while research on meteorological drivers of dengue transmission has grown significantly since 2020, it does not align with the current disease burden or long-term trends in incidence rates. The research, which evaluated 290 publications, revealed that scientific productivity is heavily concentrated in a few well-resourced nations, despite the widespread transmission of dengue across 90 countries and territories. The findings highlight a geographic mismatch in research efforts, leaving several regions with high transmission potential and severe public health burdens underrepresented in international scientific publications. The study underscores the complex interplay between climate variables and dengue transmission dynamics, emphasizing the need for more equitable research distribution to address global health challenges effectively.
Original source text (English)
HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER Thursday, November 26, 2026 No Result View All Result Login HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER No Result View All Result No Result View All Result Home NEWS Science News Health Dengue Climate Research Aligns With Current Burden but Not Long-Term Trends by November 23, 2026 in Health Reading Time: 6 mins read 0 Dengue Climate Research Aligns With Current Burden but Not Long-Term Trends Share on FacebookShare on TwitterShare on LinkedinShare on RedditShare on Telegram Scientific research examining the convergence of dengue fever and extreme weather has expanded substantially across the globe, yet national publication outputs correspond only modestly with contemporary disease burden and fail to reflect long-term increases in incidence rates. A comprehensive bibliometric investigation evaluating global literature published between 2008 and May 2026 demonstrated that while academic investigations into meteorological drivers of dengue transmission grew markedly after 2020, research productivity remains heavily concentrated within a small cluster of well-resourced nations. Led by investigator teams examining records indexed in the Web of Science Core Collection, the analysis evaluated how country-level research outputs correspond to real-world epidemiological burdens drawn from the Global Burden of Disease 2023 dataset and mechanistic indicators of climate-driven vector suitability. The resulting findings establish that while global investigative efforts are positively associated with current disease metrics, substantial geographic mismatches persist, leaving several nations with extensive transmission suitability and severe public health burdens entirely unrepresented in internationally indexed scientific publications. Dengue fever represents one of the most rapidly spreading mosquito-borne viral infections worldwide, posing acute public health threats throughout tropical and subtropical regions. Primarily transmitted to humans by the vector species Aedes aegypti and Aedes albopictus, the virus has expanded across expanding ecological ranges influenced by urbanization, human population mobility, and environmental shifts. Global health monitoring reported that by late April 2024, more than 7.6 million dengue cases were documented worldwide within a single four-month period, encompassing roughly 3.4 million laboratory-confirmed cases, over 16,000 severe clinical presentations, and upwards of 3,000 fatalities, with active transmission recorded across 90 countries and territories. In parallel, climate change has been identified as a critical driver altering viral transmission dynamics, with the Intergovernmental Panel on Climate Change affirming with high confidence that meteorological variables including ambient temperature, precipitation regimes, relative humidity, and extreme weather phenomena exert powerful regulatory influences on disease ecology. The underlying biological mechanisms linking weather extremes to dengue transmission operate through both direct vector physiology and broader environmental disruptions. Elevated ambient temperatures accelerate the metabolic processes of vector mosquitoes, shortening the extrinsic incubation period required for viral replication inside the mosquito, while simultaneously increasing biting frequency and expanding geographic regions environmentally capable of sustaining vector populations. Conversely, extreme hydrometeorological events, including prolonged heatwaves, heavy rainfall, typhoons, flooding, and droughts, disrupt mosquito breeding habitats and shift human exposure patterns in complex, nonlinear manners. Large-scale periodic climatic oscillations, such as the El Niño–Southern Oscillation, further introduce temporal and interannual variations in viral incidence across entire continents. Discover more Check Symptoms Track Weather Check Forecasts Find Treatments Track Outbreaks To rigorously characterize how the global scientific community has investigated these complex interactions, researchers executed a standardized bibliometric query across the Web of Science Core Collection on May 22, 2026. Restricting the evaluation to English-language research articles and reviews published between 2008 and 2026, the systematic screening pipeline identified 796 initial candidate records. Following the exclusion of non-English records, non-peer-reviewed document types such as conference abstracts, book chapters, and editorial notes, and 460 publications that lacked direct substantive links between dengue transmission and meteorological exposures, a finalized corpus of 290 publications was isolated for detailed quantitative synthesis. Analytical processing was carried out using Microsoft Excel 2024, VOSviewer version 1.6.20, CiteSpace version 6.4.2, and the bibliometrix package within R version 4.5.2 to trace annual publication growth, institutional collaboration structures, author co-citation networks, keyword co-occurrence groupings, and journal citation pathways. Annual research production over the eighteen-year evaluation period exhibited a steady upward trajectory characterized by distinct expansion phases. Between 2008 and 2012, annual output remained comparatively low, ranging between 5 and 12 publications per year, before expanding to 13 papers in 2013 and climbing to 22 by 2019. The post-2020 period sustained heightened productivity, reaching 24 publications in 2022 and achieving an annual peak of 28 publications in 2025. Because the database query terminated on May 22, 2026, the final year reflected an incomplete reporting window rather than an actual drop in investigational activity. Across the entire 290-article corpus, participating author affiliations encompassed 66 countries and generated 551 distinct country-publication combinations under a full-counting paradigm, wherein every nation contributing to a multi-author paper received a single publication credit. Discover more Read Med Journals Donate to Research Find Conferences Geographic analysis revealed that international research productivity in dengue and extreme weather is concentrated within a very small set of countries with advanced research infrastructures. The United States contributed to 113 publications, representing 39.0 percent of the total literature corpus, followed by China with 54 publications or 18.6 percent, the United Kingdom with 47 publications accounting for 16.2 percent, Australia with 38 publications representing 13.1 percent, and Brazil with 25 publications or 8.6 percent. International co-authorship networks demonstrated that the United States served as a central collaborative hub, linking research teams across China, Australia, the United Kingdom, Brazil, and several partner institutions in Europe and Asia. However, despite these major international links, dozens of dengue-endemic territories with lower absolute research outputs maintained sparse collaborative connections or remained completely absent from global collaboration graphs. Institutional and financial support analyses reinforced this observed structural concentration. The University of London led global academic output with 33 institutional contributions, followed closely by the London School of Hygiene and Tropical Medicine with 30 contributions, the Pennsylvania Commonwealth System of Higher Education with 26, Pennsylvania State University with 25, and the National University of Singapore with 24. Funding agency acknowledgments, recorded across 222 of the 290 publications, demonstrated that US federal entities were the leading sponsors of indexed research: the National Institutes of Health was acknowledged in 25 publications, representing 11.3 percent of funded works, followed by the National Science Foundation in 19 papers, the National Natural Science Foundation of China in 18 papers, the UK Medical Research Council in 16 papers, and the US Centers for Disease Control and Prevention in 9 papers. These reporting frequencies illustrate national investment profiles, although acknowledgment tallies do not quantify monetary allocations or prove that grant receipt directly caused elevated publication productivity. To evaluate whether research activity aligned with public health needs, investigators linked country-level publication counts with epidemiological indicators from the Global Burden of Disease 2023 study across 80 nations operationally defined as dengue-endemic based on the CDC Yellow Book criteria. Statistical evaluations utilizing Spearman rank correlations demonstrated modest but statistically significant positive associations between national publication volume and contemporary disease burden, yielding a coefficient of rho equals 0.32 (P = 0.004, n = 80) for the 2023 age-standardized incidence rate and rho equals 0.36 (P < 0.001, n = 80) for the 2023 age-standardized disability-adjusted life-year rate. Sensitivity testing confirming these results across the lower and upper bounds of the 95 percent uncertainty intervals for disability-adjusted life-year estimates yielded consistent positive correlations of rho equals 0.40 (P < 0.001) and rho equals 0.31 (P = 0.005), respectively, verifying that the positive alignment with current burden was robust to modeling uncertainty. A critical divergence emerged, however, when researchers evaluated the relationship between national scientific productivity and the long-term trajectory of disease spread. The association between country publication output and the estimated annual percentage change in age-standardized incidence rates between 1990 and 2023 was weak and failed to achieve statistical significance, exhibiting a Spearman rank correlation coefficient of rho equals 0.20 (P = 0.070, n = 80). This finding demonstrates that countries experiencing the most rapid multi-decade accelerations in dengue transmission have not received proportionally expanded scientific attention. National publication volume was more closely aligned with the contemporary level of dengue burden than with its long-term temporal trend. The study further incorporated index-P, a mechanistic indicator derived from microclimatic temperature and humidity variables that quantifies the environmental suitability of local conditions for mosquito-borne viral transmission. When evaluating 63 dengue-endemic nations possessing complete reporting data across disability-adjusted life-year rates, mean index-P values from 1990 to 2022, and Web of Science publication tallies, substantial geographic disparities became evident without constructing artificial composite scores. Several dengue-endemic countries combined relatively high disability-adjusted life-year rates and high long-term climatic suitability with few or no eligible Web of Science-indexed publications. Because index-P captures climate-driven suitability rather than observed transmission—which depends on vector presence, human mobility, and healthcare infrastructure—the researchers emphasized that these descriptive comparisons highlight regional under-representation rather than definitive proof that local scientific investigations were absent. Disciplinary and thematic mapping confirmed that the intellectual foundation of dengue and extreme weather research has evolved from isolated meteorological observations into an integrated, interdisciplinary framework. Lead publishing venues included PLOS Neglected Tropical Diseases with 28 articles, the International Journal of Environmental Research and Public Health and Parasites and Vectors with 14 papers each, Acta Tropica with 11, and the Journal of Medical Entomology and Scientific Reports with 9 each. Co-citation analyses and dual-map overlays demonstrated that citing journals rooted in clinical medicine and molecular biology draw heavily upon foundational cited literature in environmental sciences, nursing, and general public health. Author networks led by Rachel Lowe with 11 publications and frequently co-cited scholars including Simon Hales with 102 co-citations established that early keyword foci on rainfall, temperature, and localized time-series analysis have increasingly shifted toward predictive transmission modeling, urbanization risks, hydrometeorological hazards, and climate-informed early warning systems. The authors outlined essential constraints inherent to their bibliometric methodology. Restricting the literature search strictly to English-language articles and reviews indexed within the Web of Science Core Collection omitted relevant investigations published in regional databases, non-indexed journals, languages other than English, surveillance reports, or grey literature, which may have disproportionately excluded locally published research and underestimated the contributions of some dengue-endemic countries. Furthermore, publication counts were not normalized against national population sizes or total national research expenditures, and bibliometric metrics reflect publication frequency rather than study quality or causal validity. Despite these limitations, the evidence underscores that while scientific attention toward dengue and extreme weather is expanding, international partnerships, surveillance infrastructure, and local research investments must be directed toward under-represented endemic settings facing high climatic suitability and rapidly escalating epidemiological burdens. Kazakhstan reports 14 imported cases of dengue fever since start of 2026 - Qazinform

News Brief
Kazakhstan has reported 14 imported cases of dengue fever since the start of 2026, according to Qazinform. These cases were identified in individuals returning from countries such as the Maldives, Vietnam, India, Dubai, and Indonesia. The Ministry of Health confirmed all cases through laboratory testing and emphasized the importance of taking precautionary measures when traveling to regions where infectious diseases are prevalent. No new cases were reported in November, and the situation remains under monitoring. While the government has issued guidance, there is no detailed information on containment efforts or the spread of the disease within the country. The focus is on preventing further importation and ensuring public awareness.

The patients had traveled to African countries, including Uganda, Ghana, Gabon and Zanzibar. In one case, the patient arrived from Vietnam, Beissenova said. She also urged Kazakhstanis to prepare in advance when traveling to countries where infectious diseases are reported and to observe precautionary measures. 文章提到了越南河内和宣光省的病例。它强调 登革热 正在影响成年人,包括没有潜在健康问题的年轻健康个体。
事件
登革热 呈上升趋势,病例和死亡人数令人震惊。文章讨论了该疾病日益复杂的情况以及严重并发症的风险,包括休克、严重出血、肝肾损伤以及多器官衰竭。
规模、影响与风险
2026年前10个月,越南记录了超过170,000例登革热病例,较2025年同期增加了1.3倍,包括21例死亡。该疾病变得更为复杂,即使在发热消退后也可能出现严重并发症。
应对、状态与不确定性
健康专家和医生建议公众采取积极措施预防登革热,例如消除积水源、消灭蚊子幼虫和避免蚊虫叮咬。他们还强调早期医疗护理和监测预警信号的重要性。该文章是联合摘要,无法提取完整的原始文章正文。

Take proactive measures to prevent the disease and be vigilant for dengue fever complications. - Vietnam.vn

News Brief
The article published on Vietnam.vn on 2026-11-22 highlights the rising cases of dengue fever in Vietnam, with over 170,000 reported in the first 10 months of 2026, a 1.3-fold increase compared to 2025, and 21 deaths. Dengue fever is now affecting adults, including previously healthy individuals, and can lead to severe complications such as shock, liver and kidney damage, and multi-organ failure. These complications often occur when the fever subsides, typically between the 3rd and 7th day of illness. Health experts warn that many people misjudge the severity of the disease by focusing solely on platelet count, and emphasize the need for vigilance in monitoring symptoms like severe abdominal pain, frequent vomiting, and signs of bleeding. Preventive measures include eliminating stagnant water, destroying mosquito larvae, and avoiding mosquito bites. The article is a syndicated summary, and the full original article body could not be extracted.
Original source text (English)
English Login Homepage Topic News Political System Destination Event Travel Happy Vietnam Enterprise Product Heritage Museum Figure Multimedia Data Home Culture – Society Y tế Take proactive measures to prevent the disease and be vigilant for dengue fever complications. Dengue fever is on the rise, with alarming numbers of cases and deaths. Notably, this disease is no longer just a concern for young children or those with underlying health conditions. Hà Nội Mới•22/11/2026 Follow Vietnam.vn on Google Add Vietnam.vn on Google 0 Caring for a dengue fever patient with severe symptoms at the National Hospital for Tropical Diseases. Photo: Provided by the hospital. In reality, many adults, including young, healthy individuals without underlying health conditions, still experience shock, severe bleeding, liver and kidney damage, and multi-organ failure. In particular, dangerous complications can arise at the very moment when the illness seems to be subsiding, when the patient's fever begins to subside. Do n't wait for complications to arise before seeking medical attention. Khám phá thêm Tìm hiểu lịch sử Đọc Mẹo Du Lịch Khám phá ĐNA According to the Department of Disease Prevention ( Ministry of Health ), in the first 10 months of 2026, the whole country recorded more than 170,000 cases of dengue fever, a 1.3-fold increase compared to the same period in 2025, including 21 deaths. Not only has the number of cases increased, but the disease is also becoming more complex. Many adults, even young people who were previously completely healthy, have fallen into critical condition and require intensive treatment due to shock, blood clotting disorders, and multi-organ damage. A typical case is patient PL (23 years old, from Hanoi), who was previously completely healthy but was hospitalized after 7 days of high fever, chills, headache, abdominal pain, vomiting, and diarrhea. Despite treatment at another medical facility, her condition worsened. Upon transfer to the National Hospital for Tropical Diseases, the patient's platelet count had dropped to 21 G/L, liver enzymes were elevated, and she had severe liver damage. Another case involves patient LTS (23 years old, from Tuyen Quang province), who was previously healthy but quickly deteriorated after contracting dengue fever. The patient suffered from hypotension, respiratory failure, liver and kidney damage, and blood clotting disorders. Doctors had to use an extracorporeal oxygenation (ECMO) system combined with hemodialysis to save the patient's life. Based on the severe cases mentioned above, Dr. Pham Van Phuc, Deputy Director of the Intensive Care Unit (National Hospital for Tropical Diseases), noted that one of the reasons patients easily misjudge the severity of dengue fever is overemphasizing the low platelet count. This is a common symptom, but the severity of the disease cannot be determined solely based on the platelet count. Assessing the risk of complications must be based on the overall clinical condition and many other indicators, such as signs of bleeding, blood pressure, pulse, urine output, blood concentration, and organ damage. You may also like According to Dr. Pham Van Phuc, dengue fever has a particularly dangerous characteristic that many people often overlook: the disease can worsen precisely when the fever begins to subside. The critical phase usually occurs between the 3rd and 7th day after onset. At this time, the patient may experience plasma leakage, severe bleeding, or organ damage. If severe plasma leakage is not detected and treated promptly, circulatory volume can decrease, leading to shock, multi-organ failure, and death. Dr. Huynh Tran An Khuong, a medical specialist at the VNVC Vaccination System, also warned that many people are still complacent, believing that dengue fever is only dangerous for young children, so they self-medicate at home or delay seeking medical attention. In particular, when the fever subsides, patients easily mistakenly believe they have recovered, while this could be the time when the fever is subsiding. The disease is starting to become dangerous. Eliminate mosquitoes, prevent the risk of serious illness. According to health experts, dengue fever is now tending to circulate year-round in many localities, instead of being concentrated in a specific epidemic season as before. Notably, all four dengue virus types that cause the disease, including DENV-1, DENV-2, DENV-3, and DENV-4, are recorded as circulating. The simultaneous presence of multiple virus types means that the risk of infection, reinfection, and complicated disease progression is always present. Dr. Huynh Tran An Khuong noted that people who have previously had dengue fever can still be reinfected because long-term immunity is mainly formed against the specific virus type they were infected with, while cross-protection against other types is usually temporary. In particular, when reinfected with a different virus type, patients may face a higher risk of more severe illness due to antibody-dependent enhancement (ADE). However, not all cases of reinfection lead to a serious illness. Besides being vigilant about the risk of reinfection, people also need to recognize the correct progression of the disease to avoid missing the right time for intervention. Dr. Pham Van Phuc noted that people with or suspected of having dengue fever should immediately go to a medical facility if they experience warning signs such as severe or continuous abdominal pain, frequent vomiting, lethargy, restlessness, nosebleeds, bleeding gums, decreased urination, cold hands and feet, or extreme fatigue. These are symptoms that should not be self-monitored at home, as they may indicate a worsening condition and require prompt assessment and treatment by a doctor. For cases where home monitoring and treatment are prescribed by a doctor, patients need to rest, drink plenty of fluids, and closely monitor their temperature, appetite, urine output, and any unusual symptoms. Medication use must also strictly follow professional instructions; under no circumstances should you increase the dosage of fever-reducing medication or use anti-inflammatory drugs without a prescription, as some medications can increase the risk of bleeding in dengue fever patients. You may also like In addition, patients need to attend follow-up appointments as scheduled, undergo tests as prescribed, and maintain monitoring throughout the period of risk of worsening condition. Even when the fever subsides and symptoms improve, patients should not stop monitoring or assume they have fully recovered. Given the complex situation surrounding dengue fever, health experts advise people to proactively take preventive measures to limit the risk of community spread. Important measures include: eliminating stagnant water sources, destroying mosquito larvae, maintaining a clean living environment, and avoiding mosquito bites. In addition, dengue fever vaccination is a preventive measure that can be considered as directed by healthcare professionals and according to the regulations of the vaccination facility. The combination of mosquito control, appropriate vaccination, and early detection of warning signs will contribute to reducing the risk of contracting the disease and limiting dangerous complications. 免疫功能低下的成年人面临更高的 重症登革热 风险 - Bioengineer.org

Immunocompromised Adults Face Higher Risk of Severe Dengue - Bioengineer.org
新闻简报Bioengineer.org 于 2026 年 11 月 25 日发表的一项多中心回顾性 队列研究 研究发现,与非免疫功能低下的患者相比,被诊断患有登革热的免疫功能低下的成年人面临 重症登革热 的风险显着更高。该研究评估了泰国 2023 年 1 月至 2024 年 6 月的医院记录,结果显示,22% 的免疫功能低下患者符合 WHO 2009 年 重症登革热 标准,而非免疫功能低下患者的这一比例为 4.9%。这些人的入院率更高,住院时间更长,30 天 死亡率 也更长。该研究强调需要改进免疫功能低下成人的预防和临床管理策略。免疫功能低下状态与 重症登革热 独立相关,优势比为 3.43,而较高的查尔森合并症指数也与风险相关,优势比为 1.33。
查看新闻五要素
时间
该研究评估了2023年1月至2024年6月期间的医院记录,新闻于2026年11月25日发布。
地点与对象
该研究在泰国进行,重点关注具有特定免疫状态临床标准的免疫功能低下的成年人。
事件
据 Bioengineer.org 报道,一项多中心回顾性 队列研究 研究发现,与非免疫功能低下的患者相比,被诊断患有登革热的免疫功能低下的成年人患 重症登革热 的风险要高得多。
规模、影响与风险
根据 WHO 2009 年标准,免疫功能低下患者的 重症登革热 发生率为 22%,而非免疫功能低下个体的 重症登革热 率为 4.9%。他们的住院率也更高,住院时间更长,30 天 死亡率 也更长。
应对、状态与不确定性
该研究的结果发表在一篇新闻文章中,研究人员得出结论,需要改进免疫功能低下成年人的预防和临床管理策略。该证据基于回顾性 队列研究 数据。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Immunocompromised Adults Face Higher Risk of Severe Dengue - Bioengineer.org

News Brief
A multicenter retrospective cohort study published on November 25, 2026, by Bioengineer.org found that immunocompromised adults diagnosed with dengue face a significantly higher risk of severe dengue compared to non-immunocompromised patients. The study, which evaluated hospital records from January 2023 to June 2024 in Thailand, revealed that 22% of immunocompromised individuals met WHO 2009 criteria for severe dengue, compared to 4.9% of non-immunocompromised patients. These individuals also had higher rates of hospital admission, longer stays, and increased 30-day mortality. The research highlights the need for improved prevention and clinical management strategies for immunocompromised adults. Immunocompromised status was independently associated with severe dengue, with an odds ratio of 3.43, while a higher Charlson comorbidity index also contributed to risk, with an odds ratio of 1.33.
Original source text (English)
HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER Thursday, November 26, 2026 No Result View All Result Login HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER No Result View All Result No Result View All Result Home NEWS Science News Health Immunocompromised Adults Face Higher Risk of Severe Dengue by November 25, 2026 in Health Reading Time: 2 mins read 0 Immunocompromised Adults Face Higher Risk of Severe Dengue Share on FacebookShare on TwitterShare on LinkedinShare on RedditShare on Telegram In a multicenter retrospective cohort study examining laboratory-confirmed dengue infections, researchers found that immunocompromised adults faced a substantially higher risk of developing severe dengue compared to non-immunocompromised patients. The investigation evaluated hospital records between January 2023 and June 2024 to address the lack of large comparative cohorts evaluating dengue severity in individuals with compromised immune defenses. The study included 894 patients diagnosed with dengue, of whom 59 met specific clinical criteria for immunocompromised status. Qualifying conditions included living with HIV with CD4 counts below 350 cells per microliter, having received a solid organ transplant or a hematopoietic stem cell transplant within the preceding two years, active malignancy, asplenia or splenectomy, and medical regimens requiring immunosuppressive medications. Demographic analysis revealed that immunocompromised patients were significantly older, presenting with a median age of 47.0 years compared to 33.5 years in the non-immunocompromised group. Additionally, these patients carried a higher baseline burden of preexisting health conditions, reflected in a median Charlson comorbidity index of 1 versus 0 among non-immunocompromised individuals. While primary clinical features at initial presentation were largely similar, an absence of fever was noted more frequently in the immunocompromised cohort, occurring in 10.2 percent compared to 1.1 percent. Routine laboratory values appeared comparable upon admission, yet immunocompromised patients subsequently exhibited significantly lower nadir platelet counts and prolonged thrombocytopenia throughout their illness. Primary clinical endpoints showed marked disparities, with severe dengue according to World Health Organization 2009 criteria documented in 22 percent of immunocompromised individuals compared to only 4.9 percent of non-immunocompromised patients. Patients with impaired immunity also experienced higher rates of hospital admission, longer stays, and increased 30-day mortality. In multivariable analysis, immunocompromised status and comorbidity index were both independently associated with severe dengue, yielding an odds ratio of 3.43 for immune impairment and 1.33 for the comorbidity score. The researchers concluded that these findings provide critical evidence demonstrating an elevated risk of severe outcomes, highlighting the urgent need for improved strategies for prevention and clinical management in this population.
